<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the normal projective base of a projective Lagrangian fibration from a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold is projective space. This resolves the projective-space base conjecture for such fibrations in every dimension and deformation type.

- **claim-main-projective-space-base** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $n\geq1$, and let $X$ be a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold of dimension $2n$, with symplectic form $\sigma$. Suppose that $$f:X\longrightarrow B$$ is a projective surjective holomorphic morphism with connected fibers, where $B$ is a normal projective variety of dimension $n$. Assume that every irreducible component of every fiber has dimension $n$ and that the restriction of $\sigma$ to its smooth locus is zero. Then $B$ is isomorphic to $\mathbb P^n_{\mathbb C}$.
  - Explanation: The normal projective base of a projective Lagrangian fibration from a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold is projective space, with no smoothness or deformation-type restriction.
- **claim-background-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-background)): In the setting of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the following hold.* 1. *$X$ is projective, $e\neq0$ is nef and $q(e)=0$. The base is $\mathbb Q$-factorial and klt, has Picard number one, and $-K_B$ is ample as a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor. A smooth fiber is an abelian variety after choosing an origin.* 2. *There are reflexive identifications $$R^j f_*\mathcal O_X\simeq\Omega_B^{[j]}.$$ For every integral Weil divisor $A$ ample as a $\mathbb Q$-divisor, $$H^i\!\left(B, (\Omega_B^{[j]}\otimes\mathcal O_B(A))^{**}\right)=0 \quad(i>0,\ j>0).$$ The reflexivity assertion also applies locally to the projective equidimensional fibrations with connected fibers, smooth source $Z$ satisfying $\omega_Z\simeq\mathcal O_Z$, and normal Cohen–Macaulay $\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein target that occur below.* 3. *The rational intersection cohomology of $B$ has the Betti numbers of $\mathbb P^n$, and the restriction of $H^2(X,\mathbb Q)$ to a smooth fiber has one-dimensional image.* 4. *If $B$ is smooth, then $B\simeq\mathbb P^n$.
  - Explanation: Collects the known structure of the given fibration: projectivity, Fano-type klt base, reflexive identification of higher direct images with reflexive differentials, projective-space Betti numbers, and projective space when the base is smooth.
- **claim-tangent-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-tangent-rigidity)): If $n>1$, then $H^1(B,T_B)=0$.
  - Explanation: The first cohomology of the tangent sheaf of the base vanishes when the base dimension exceeds one.
- **bound-second-betti-at-least-four** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-betti-bound)): One has $b_2(X)\geq4$.
  - Explanation: The second Betti number of the total space is bounded below by a hyperbolic plane together with the real period plane.
- **claim-strong-syz-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-strong-syz)): Let $Y$ be a compact IHS Kähler manifold of dimension $2m$. If a holomorphic line bundle $L$ satisfies $$c_1(L)\neq0,\qquad c_1(L)\in\overline{\mathcal K_Y},\qquad q_Y(c_1(L))=0,$$ then a positive tensor power of $L$ is generated by its global sections. Its associated map with connected fibers has a normal projective base of dimension $m$. Every component of every fiber has dimension $m$ and is Lagrangian.
  - Explanation: Companion input: a nonzero nef isotropic line bundle on a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold is semiample and defines an equidimensional Lagrangian fibration over a normal projective base.
- **claim-finite-smooth-domination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-smooth-domination)): Every point of $B$ has an algebraic neighborhood, after an étale base change if necessary, admitting a finite surjection from a smooth variety of dimension $n$. In particular, every analytic germ of $B$ is finitely dominated by a smooth germ of the same dimension. Every such germ is analytically $\mathbb Q$-factorial.
  - Explanation: Every germ of the base is finitely dominated by a smooth germ of the same dimension, which makes it analytically Q-factorial.
- **claim-rational-smoothness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-rational-smoothness)): The space $B$ is an oriented rational homology manifold of real dimension $2n$. Its rational intersection complex is $\mathbb Q_B[n]$. Consequently its ordinary rational cohomology has the Betti numbers of $\mathbb P^n$. The local rational homology-manifold assertion holds for every normal germ finitely dominated by a smooth germ, in particular for the maximal charts constructed below.
  - Explanation: The base is an oriented rational homology manifold whose intersection complex is the shifted constant sheaf, so its rational cohomology is that of projective space.
- **claim-maximal-quasi-etale-charts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-maximal-charts)): Every pointed germ $(B,b)$ has a connected finite normal Galois quasi-étale cover $(C,c)$ which dominates every connected finite quasi-étale cover of that germ. The chart $(C,c)$ is klt, is finitely dominated by a smooth germ, and has no nontrivial connected finite quasi-étale cover. The finite diagrams can be defined over an algebraic étale neighborhood of $b$.* *More generally, let $U\to B$ be an integral separated étale algebraic neighborhood and let $\pi:C\to U$ be an integral finite normal quasi-étale cover. Put $X_U=X\times_B U$. The base change $X_U\times_U C$ has a unique irreducible component dominating $C$; let $Z$ be its normalization and $h:Z\to C$ its structure map. Then $h$ is projective and equidimensional of relative dimension $n$, has connected fibers, and satisfies $h_*\mathcal O_Z=\mathcal O_C$. The finite map $p:Z\to X_U$ is étale. Consequently $Z$ is smooth, the pullback of the symplectic form on $X$ is symplectic on $Z$, its canonical bundle is trivial, and the fibers of $h$ are Lagrangian. If $\pi$ is Galois with group $\Gamma$, then $p$ is a $\Gamma$-torsor. Ordinary étale base change on $C$ preserves projectivity, equidimensionality, connected fibers, the equality $h_*\mathcal O_Z=\mathcal O_C$, and the smooth symplectic source. The torsor in the statement belongs to the original finite chart.
  - Explanation: Every germ has a maximal Galois quasi-étale chart that is klt and finitely dominated by a smooth germ, with a normalized smooth symplectic source chart.
- **claim-trace-ideal-is-radical** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-trace-radical)): Let $(R,\mathfrak m)$ be any noetherian normal henselian local $\mathbb C$-algebra with residue field $\mathbb C$. For finite reflexive modules $M,N$, define $$\mathcal R(M,N)= \{a\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_R(M,N):\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(ba)\in\mathfrak m \text{ for every }b\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_R(N,M)\}.$$ These spaces form a tensor ideal. For every $M$, $\mathcal R(M,M)$ is the Jacobson radical of the finite $R$-algebra $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_R(M)$. The quotient category is semisimple abelian over $\mathbb C$, has finite-dimensional Hom spaces, and its categorical dimensions are the positive integer ranks of its nonzero objects.
  - Explanation: Over a normal henselian local algebra the trace ideal is the Jacobson radical of endomorphisms, and the quotient category is semisimple abelian.
- **claim-split-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-split-lift)): Over any ring in Lemma <a href="#loc:trace-radical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:trace-radical">9</a>, if a morphism of reflexive modules becomes a split injection in the trace quotient, it is already a split injection of reflexive modules. An isomorphism in the quotient lifts to an isomorphism. In particular, an object is a sum of copies of the tensor unit in the quotient if and only if its underlying module is free.
  - Explanation: A morphism that is a split injection in the trace quotient is already a split injection of reflexive modules, so triviality in the quotient means freeness.
- **claim-residual-tensor-group** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-tannakian)): The residual category $\overline{\mathcal C}=\mathcal C/\mathcal R$ admits a fiber functor $\omega$ to complex vector spaces. For a choice of $\omega$, tensor reconstruction identifies it with $\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb C}(G)$, where $G=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}^\otimes(\omega)$ is a reductive algebraic subgroup of $\operatorname{GL}(V_{\mathrm{fib}})$ and $V_{\mathrm{fib}}=\omega(\overline V)$. This representation is faithful and has dimension $n$.
  - Explanation: The residual category has a fiber functor identifying it with representations of a faithful reductive group on a space whose dimension is the base dimension.
- **claim-connection-gives-freeness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-connection-free)): If $a(M_U)=0$, then $M$ is a free $R$-module.
  - Explanation: Vanishing of the Atiyah class on the smooth locus of a maximal chart forces the reflexive module to be free.
- **claim-atiyah-tensor-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-atiyah-tests)): Let $M$ be an object, and let $t_j$ be actual sections of finitely many functorial mixed tensor powers $T_j(M)$ of $M$ and $M^\vee$ on the chart. Direct sums and the usual symmetric or alternating projectors may be used; each construction carries the natural action of every endomorphism of $M$. Put $Q=\bigoplus_jT_j(M)$ and define $$\Phi_M:\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{End}}}(M)\longrightarrow Q, \qquad A\longmapsto(A\cdot t_j)_j$$ by the infinitesimal tensor action. The coefficient map for the Atiyah class is the morphism in $\mathcal C$ $$\label{loc:psi-coefficient} \Psi_M=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_V\widehat\otimes\Phi_M: V\widehat\otimes\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{End}}}(M) \longrightarrow V\widehat\otimes Q.$$ On the smooth locus its ordinary bundle restriction satisfies $(\Psi_{M,U})_*a(M_U)=0$ in $H^1(U,(V\widehat\otimes Q)_U)$. If $\overline{\Psi_M}$ is injective in $\overline{\mathcal C}$, then $a(M_U)=0$.
  - Explanation: Injectivity of the tensor-test coefficient map in the residual category implies the Atiyah class vanishes on the smooth locus.
- **claim-group-connected-semisimple** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-connected-semisimple)): The reductive group $G$ of Proposition <a href="#loc:tannakian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:tannakian">11</a> is connected and semisimple.
  - Explanation: The residual tensor group is connected and semisimple, because finite-image representations are trivial and characters have finite order.
- **claim-block-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-block-obstruction)): Lift a decomposition of $V_{\mathrm{fib}}$ into irreducible $G$-modules to a decomposition $V=\bigoplus_iV_i$ of reflexive modules. Let $$\mathfrak h_i= \mathfrak g\cap\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(V_{i,\mathrm{fib}}) \subset\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(V_{\mathrm{fib}}), \qquad\mathfrak g=\operatorname{Lie}(G),$$ where the endomorphisms in this intersection are zero on every other block. Regard $\mathfrak h_i$ on $T_i=V_{i,\mathrm{fib}}^\vee$ by the dual action. If $\mathfrak h_i^{(1)}=0$, then $V_i$ is free. Consequently every nontrivial irreducible block has $\mathfrak h_i^{(1)}\ne0$.
  - Explanation: A block with vanishing first prolongation is free, so every nontrivial irreducible block has nonzero first prolongation.
- **claim-auxiliary-factor-kills-prolongation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-auxiliary-factor)): Suppose $T=U\otimes W$, $\dim W>1$, and $\mathfrak h\subset\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(U)\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_W$. Then $\mathfrak h^{(1)}=0$.
  - Explanation: An auxiliary tensor factor of dimension greater than one forces the first prolongation to vanish.
- **claim-rank-one-symbol** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-rank-one-symbol)): Let $\mathfrak h\subset\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(T)$ be semisimple, faithful and irreducible. If $\mathfrak h^{(1)}\ne0$, then $\mathfrak h$ contains an endomorphism of rank one.
  - Explanation: For a semisimple faithful irreducible Lie algebra, nonzero first prolongation produces an endomorphism of rank one.
- **claim-rank-one-classification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-rank-one-classification)): A faithful irreducible representation $T$ of a complex semisimple Lie algebra containing a rank-one endomorphism is the standard representation, or its dual, of $\mathfrak{sl}(T)$, or the standard representation of $\mathfrak{sp}(T)$. In particular the Lie algebra is simple.
  - Explanation: A faithful irreducible representation containing a rank-one endomorphism is a standard special linear or symplectic representation, and the algebra is simple.
- **claim-slsp-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-slsp)): For a maximal chart there is a decomposition $$V_{\mathrm{fib}}={\bf1}^{\oplus s}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^r L_i, \qquad G=\prod_{i=1}^rG_i, \qquad G_i=\operatorname{SL}(L_i)\ \text{or}\ \operatorname{Sp}(L_i), \label{loc:group-product}$$ where each $G_i$ acts by its standard representation on $L_i$ and trivially on all other summands. There are no repeated nontrivial irreducible summands. We place $\operatorname{SL}_2=\operatorname{Sp}_2$ in the symplectic list. The integer $s$ is the number of free rank-one direct summands of $V$.
  - Explanation: The residual group is a product of special linear and symplectic groups acting by standard representations on distinct nontrivial summands.
- **claim-exterior-constituent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-exterior-constituent)): In the notation of Theorem <a href="#loc:slsp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:slsp">19</a>, suppose $r>0$ and put $W=\bigoplus_{i=1}^rL_i$. There are a simple $G$-module $E$ and an integer $1\leq j_0\leq\dim W$ such that, for $0\leq b\leq\dim W$, $$\dim_{\mathbb C}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_G(E,\Lambda^bW)= \begin{cases}1,&b=j_0,\\0,&b\ne j_0.\end{cases}$$ The multiplicity of $E$ in $\Lambda^{j_0}V_{\mathrm{fib}}$ is also one. The selection of $E$ is intrinsic to the standard factors and is preserved by permutations of the factors.
  - Explanation: There is a simple module occurring once in a single exterior degree of the nontrivial part, intrinsic under permutation of factors.
- **claim-evaluation-and-smooth-factors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-str-evaluation)): The largest rank of a free direct summand of $\Omega_V^{[1]}{}_v$ is $r(v)$. There is an analytic product decomposition $$\label{str:analytic-product} (V,v)\simeq(\Delta^{r(v)},0)\times(T,t),$$ where all vector fields on $(T,t)$ have zero evaluation at $t$. Moreover, if $\beta$ is a reflexive one-form and a vector field $\xi$ has zero evaluation at $v$, then $\beta(\xi)(v)=0$.
  - Explanation: The largest free summand of reflexive one-forms equals the evaluation dimension of derivations, giving an analytic product with a polydisc factor.
- **bound-rank-locus-dimension** (bound, [source](#stmt-str-rank-locus)): On such a normal germ, the locus $\{v:r(v)\leq a\}$ is closed analytic and has dimension at most $a$.
  - Explanation: The locus where the evaluation rank is at most a given value is closed analytic of dimension at most that value.
- **claim-minimum-stratum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-str-minimum)): Let $d=\min_{b\in B}s(b)$. If $d=n$, every germ of $B$ is a quotient singularity. Otherwise the minimum locus is a nonempty closed algebraic subset. Each of its connected components $S_0$ is irreducible, normal and projective of dimension $d$. On a maximal chart over a point of $S_0$, its reduced inverse image is, as a germ of a pair, $$\label{str:minimum-pair} (V,S_V)\simeq(\Delta^d\times T,\Delta^d\times\{t\}).$$ At every point of $S_V$, the vector-field evaluation space is exactly the tangent space to $S_V$. There is a smooth separated Deligne–Mumford stack $\mathcal S$ with coarse space $S_0$ whose charts are these smooth factors. Its stabilizers are the full deck stabilizers, including those acting trivially on the smooth factor.
  - Explanation: Either all germs are quotient singularities or the minimum free-rank locus has normal projective components with a product pair structure and a smooth Deligne–Mumford stack.
- **claim-marked-artin-algebraization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-str-marked-artin)): Let $(V,v)$ be an algebraic complex germ, let $S\subset V$ be a reduced closed subgerm, and suppose the analytic pair is $(\Delta^d\times T,\Delta^d\times\{t\})$. There are an algebraic transverse germ $(T_0,t_0)$ and common étale neighborhoods of pairs $$\label{str:etale-product-pair} (V,S)\ \longleftarrow\ (U,S_U)\ \longrightarrow\ (\mathbb A^d\times T_0,\mathbb A^d\times\{t_0\}).$$ Both inverse-image ideals defining $S_U$ are equal, without truncation. For $k\geq1$, put $A_k=\mathcal O_{T_0,t_0}/\mathfrak m_{t_0}^k$ and let $U_k$ be defined by the $k$th power of this common ideal. After shrinking the displayed neighborhoods, there are compatible isomorphisms $$\label{str:finite-product} U_k\simeq S_U\times\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A_k \quad\text{over}\quad \mathbb A^d\times\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A_k.$$ No equivariance of these product isomorphisms is asserted.
  - Explanation: A marked analytic product pair algebraizes in étale neighborhoods keeping the marked ideal exactly, with compatible product isomorphisms on all thickenings.
- **claim-compatible-product-presentations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-str-products)): The following data exist along $S_0$.* 1. *There are finitely many normal finite Galois quasi-étale charts $V_\alpha\to B_\alpha$, with deck groups $\Gamma_\alpha$ and with $B_\alpha\to B$ separated and étale, covering $S_0$. They are maximal at every point over $S_0$.* 2. *Normalized main overlaps give an étale groupoid along the reduced inverse images $S_\alpha\subset V_\alpha$. Its quotient is the stack $\mathcal S$ of Proposition <a href="#str:minimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="str:minimum">23</a>. Writing $I$ for the marked ideal on the object charts, the same finite diagrams define, for every $k\geq1$, a proper separated Deligne–Mumford stack $\mathcal S_k$, with $\mathcal S_1=\mathcal S$. Its coarse space is finite over $B$ and projective.* 3. *A finite family of étale refinements of the object charts covers their closed strata and has the marked-product presentations of Lemma <a href="#str:marked-artin" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="str:marked-artin">24</a>. All transition maps come from the normalized ambient overlaps and preserve $I$ exactly. Their restrictions are consequently compatible for all powers $I^k$.* 4. *The normalized source charts give ordinary pullback and descent of coherent sheaves on any $\mathcal S_k$ to coherent sheaves on $X$ supported over $S_0$.* *These assertions are encoded by finitely many algebraic schemes, morphisms, ideals and identities. In particular they can be spread simultaneously before choosing any order of Frobenius pullback.
  - Explanation: Finite maximal charts, an étale groupoid, thickened Deligne–Mumford stacks and descent of coherent sheaves to the source exist along the minimal stratum.
- **claim-uniform-transverse-calculations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-str-uniform)): On the finite family of product refinements in Proposition <a href="#str:products" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="str:products">25</a>, the ambient sheaves $\mathcal N_j=\Omega^{[j]}$, the fixed natural mixed reflexive tensor expressions in them, and their ambient Hom and trace maps have fixed transverse descriptions. Their completed systems at order $k$ are obtained from fixed finite modules over $A_k$ by extension along the smooth factor. The same holds for kernels and images of the resulting fixed transverse maps. Residual retracts are obtained by applying their idempotents to the resulting barred bundles. In particular the barred Hom sheaves in <a href="#str:barred-hom" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="str:barred-hom">[str:barred-hom]</a> are locally free on $\mathcal S$ and their fibers are the local residual trace Hom spaces.* *Consequently a property of these modules and maps that, for each fixed transverse complete ring, holds at every order $k\geq k_0$ has one valid order on the whole stratum, provided it is preserved by flat scalar extension and can be checked by faithfully flat completion. The completed product descriptions persist after replacing the smooth parameters by lifts of another coordinate system on the reduced stratum.
  - Explanation: Reflexive tensor constructions along the stratum have fixed transverse descriptions, so properties holding at large order hold uniformly.
- **claim-compatible-direct-images** (theorem, [source](#stmt-str-direct-images)): Let $U\to B$ be an integral separated étale algebraic neighborhood, let $\pi:C\to U$ be an integral finite normal Galois quasi-étale chart, and let $h:Z\to C$ be the normalized source chart of Proposition <a href="#loc:maximal-charts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:maximal-charts">8</a>. For the global degreewise isomorphisms $\phi_j:R^jf_*\mathcal O_X\to\Omega_B^{[j]}$ fixed after Proposition <a href="#prop:background" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:background">2</a>, there are unique isomorphisms $$\phi_{j,C}:R^jh_*\mathcal O_Z\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\Omega_C^{[j]}, \qquad 0\leq j\leq n,$$ whose restrictions to $C^\circ=C\times_BB_{\mathrm{reg}}$ are the ordinary flat pullbacks of $\phi_j$. The degree-zero map is the unit. These isomorphisms are compatible with ordinary étale refinement and with every normalized main overlap whose projections are étale. In particular they give compatible ambient identifications $\mathcal N_{j,\alpha}=R^jh_{\alpha*}\mathcal O_{X_\alpha}$ on the charts of Proposition <a href="#str:products" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="str:products">25</a>.
  - Explanation: Higher direct images of the structure sheaf on normalized source charts are canonically identified with reflexive differentials, compatibly with refinement and overlaps.
- **claim-homotopy-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bc-homotopy-criterion)): Let $C$ be a bounded complex of $R$-modules. If there are chain maps $C\rightleftarrows D_0$ that are inverse up to chain homotopy and $d_{D_0}=0$, then $\gamma_M^i(C)$ is an isomorphism for every $R$-module $M$ and every $i$. If <a href="#bc:coefficient-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bc:coefficient-target">[bc:coefficient-target]</a> and <a href="#bc:canonical-compatibility" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bc:canonical-compatibility">[bc:canonical-compatibility]</a> hold, the canonical map $\beta_M^i$ is therefore an isomorphism.
  - Explanation: A homotopy retract onto a zero-differential complex gives coefficient base change for every module without flatness.
- **claim-finite-regular-algebras-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bc-finite-modules)): Let $L$ be a complete noetherian normal local domain, and let $A_1,A_2$ be finite dominant local $L$-algebras that are regular local rings. Put $d_i=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}_L A_i$. If these rings have positive characteristic and arise by completing local rings of varieties over a perfect field, then $$\label{bc:regular-comparison} A_1^{\oplus d_2}\simeq A_2^{\oplus d_1} \qquad\text{as $L$-modules}.$$ The same conclusion holds in characteristic zero when the diagram is the completion of an algebraic finite diagram at a closed point over $\mathbb C$. Finite products of the indicated local algebras can also be compared, using their total generic ranks.
  - Explanation: Two finite regular local algebras over a complete normal domain become isomorphic as modules after scaling by their generic ranks.
- **claim-uniform-twist-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bc-uniform-twists)): Let $R$ be noetherian, let $\mathsf H$ be an algebra finite over its central subring $R$, and let $\mathcal P$ on $Y=\mathbb P^n_R$ be locally finite projective as a right $\mathsf H_Y$-module. There is an integer $m_0$ such that, for every $m\ge m_0$ and every left $\mathsf H$-module $Q$, $$\begin{aligned} H^j(Y,\mathcal P(m)\otimes_{\mathsf H}Q)&=0 &&(j>0),\label{bc:uniform-vanishing}\\ H^0(Y,\mathcal P(m))\otimes_{\mathsf H}Q &\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } H^0(Y,\mathcal P(m)\otimes_{\mathsf H}Q). \label{bc:uniform-sections} \end{aligned}$$ The arrow is the canonical multiplication map, and $H^0(Y,\mathcal P(m))$ is a finite projective right $\mathsf H$-module. The bound is independent of $Q$.
  - Explanation: One twist bound gives vanishing of higher cohomology and global-section base change for all coefficient modules at once.
- **claim-differential-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bc-differential-identity)): For the fixed $g\ne0$ in <a href="#bc:cleared-homotopy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bc:cleared-homotopy">[bc:cleared-homotopy]</a>, on a finite principal-open cover of $P$ there are finitely many $t_j\in R$ and algebraic differential operators $D_j$ with regular coefficients on the respective open sets such that $$\label{bc:differential-equation} \eta=\sum_j D_j(g t_j\eta).$$ The identity is in the middle relative de Rham module on the generic smooth locus, but its operators are regular also at the other points of the chosen open sets. For one integer $o_g$ fixed in characteristic zero, they have expressions $$\label{bc:operator-bound} D_j=\sum_{\|\alpha\|\le o_g}c_{j,\alpha}(u)\partial_u^\alpha, \qquad \partial_u^\alpha= \partial_{u_1}^{\alpha_1}\cdots\partial_{u_n}^{\alpha_n},$$ where each $c_{j,\alpha}$ is regular on its specified open set.
  - Explanation: The relative volume form lies in the differential-operator module generated by multiples of the clearing factor, with operators of bounded order.
- **claim-cartier-primes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bc-cartier-primes)): There are arbitrarily large remaining residue characteristics, at degree-one places of a finite extension of the number field, for which $$\operatorname{Car}(F_*\sigma_X)\ne0.$$ On the geometric special fiber, the map $$\label{bc:cartier-splitting} \psi_X:F_*\mathcal O_X\longrightarrow\mathcal O_X, \qquad \psi_X(z)=\operatorname{Car}(F_*(z\sigma_X))/\sigma_X$$ satisfies $\psi_X(1)=\delta\in k^*$.
  - Explanation: Cartier trace of the volume form is nonzero at arbitrarily large residue characteristics, via comparison of étale and crystalline Frobenius traces and Hodge–Tate weights.
- **claim-clearing-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bc-clearing-test)): For every closed point $b\in\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$ in a retained chart, there is a function $t$ regular near $b$ such that $$\label{bc:unit-test} \psi_h(gt)(b)\ne0.$$
  - Explanation: At every closed point some function makes the Cartier pushdown of the clearing factor times it nonzero.
- **claim-ordinary-coefficient-base-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bc-ordinary)): Perform the finite characteristic-zero preparations above on a finite-type integral model. After any fixed finite enlargement of its algebraic and coherent data, and restriction to any nonempty principal open retaining the required identities and geometric properties, one can choose a characteristic-zero closed point and a number-field model with geometric reductions in arbitrarily large residue characteristics $p$ that retain these data and satisfy $$\psi_X(1)=\delta\in k^*,\qquad p>e_\mu,\quad p>o_g \quad\text{on every member of the finite chart cover}.$$ On each such reduction the following assertions hold simultaneously. On every retained source chart, for every $R$-module $M$ and every $i$, the canonical map $$\label{bc:canonical-base-change} (R^ih_*\mathcal O_Z)\otimes_RM\longrightarrow R^ih_*(h^*\widetilde M)$$ is an isomorphism, with ordinary tensor and pullback. In particular, for every coherent module $M$ supported on any finite thickening of $\mathcal S$ in $\widehat{\mathcal B}$, $$\label{bc:ordinary-euler} \chi(X,f^*M)=\sum_{i=0}^n(-1)^i \chi(\widehat{\mathcal B},N_i\otimes M).$$ One reduction works for all these $M$ and all thickening orders.
  - Explanation: In large characteristic reductions, higher direct images satisfy ordinary base change for all modules and thickenings, giving the Euler characteristic formula.
- **claim-euler-characteristic-zero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eu-euler-zero)): If a maximal chart of $B$ is singular, then the closed minimal stratum satisfies $$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S_0)=0.$$ Here $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}$ is the ordinary Euler characteristic of the coarse space.
  - Explanation: If a maximal chart of the base is singular, the ordinary Euler characteristic of the closed minimal stratum vanishes.
- **claim-exterior-isolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eu-exterior)): In the barred tensor calculus, the trivial-isotypic multiplicity bundle of $V=N_1$ is canonically $$\mathcal U=\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}(\mathcal O,V)=\Omega^1_{\mathcal S}.$$ Let $W$ be the complementary object to this evaluation summand. There are idempotents $p_b\in\overline{\mathcal E}$, supported in the $N_b$ block, whose images are $\Lambda^bW$. If $L$ is any left $\overline{\mathcal E}$-module, then $$\label{eu:block-exterior} \mathbf1_{N_i}L\simeq \bigoplus_{a+b=i}\Lambda^a\Omega^1_{\mathcal S}\otimes p_bL.$$ Moreover, there are an integer $j_0$ and an isotypic idempotent $u$ in the $N_{j_0}$ block such that $$\label{eu:isolated-constituent} u\overline{\mathcal E}p_b= \begin{cases} \mathcal O_{\mathcal S}u,&b=j_0,\\ 0,&b\ne j_0. \end{cases}$$ The identity line in this formula has trivial action under every stabilizer.
  - Explanation: In the barred calculus the trivial part of the cotangent module is the cotangent bundle of the stratum, and an exterior constituent is isolated with trivial stabilizer action.
- **claim-truncated-endomorphism-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eu-truncated-end)): For one sufficiently large integer $k$, set $$\mathcal S_k=(\widehat{\mathcal B},\mathcal O/I^k),\quad T_i=N_i/I^kN_i,\quad T=\bigoplus_iT_i.$$ There is a canonical surjection $$\label{eu:full-end-quotient} \mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{\mathcal S_k}(T)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal E}$$ with nilpotent kernel, compatible with the action of $\mathcal E$. After specializing any smooth product parameters, the image of ambient endomorphisms in the specialized $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(T)$ still surjects onto the specialized barred algebra, with nilpotent kernel.
  - Explanation: At a large thickening there is a canonical surjection from the full endomorphism algebra onto the barred algebra with nilpotent kernel.
- **claim-reference-number-vanishes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eu-reference-number)): Let $F_0=f^*\mathcal O_{\mathcal S}$, using ordinary pullback and the descended closed-subscheme structure on the source. Then $$\label{eu:reference-vanishing} \int_X [F_0]_c\,\mathop{\mathrm{td}}_{2n-c}(X)=0.$$ After restricting a finite coherent model to a nonempty principal open, the same equality holds for the corresponding reference sheaf on every geometric fiber.
  - Explanation: The leading Chern–Todd number of the pullback of the stratum structure sheaf vanishes, using the isotropic Riemann–Roch polynomial.
- **claim-tame-euler-functional** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eu-tame-functional)): Let $\mathcal D$ be a smooth proper tame Deligne–Mumford stack of pure dimension $d$ with projective coarse space. The functional $$\Theta(G)=\chi\bigl(\mathcal D, \lambda_{-1}(\Omega^1_{\mathcal D})\otimes G\bigr)$$ on vector bundles is additive, and its additive extension to $K^0(\mathcal D)$ depends only on pointwise full-stabilizer representations. There is a constant $C_{\mathcal D}$ such that $\|\Theta(G)\|\le C_{\mathcal D}\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(G)$ for every vector bundle $G$. Consequently, for any finite signed presentation $V=\sum_i a_i[G_i]$, one has $$\|\Theta(V)\|\le C_{\mathcal D}\sum_i\|a_i\|\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(G_i).$$ The latter bound uses the chosen presentation, not the virtual rank.
  - Explanation: On a tame proper Deligne–Mumford stack the exterior-twisted Euler functional is additive, depends only on stabilizer representations and is bounded linearly by rank.
- **claim-frobenius-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eu-growth)): For a fixed product module $M$ on a fixed good reduction, putting $q=p^a$ gives $$\chi(X,F_X^{a*}f^*M)=O(q^c).$$ The implicit constant may depend on the reduction and on $M$.
  - Explanation: Euler characteristics of Frobenius pullbacks of a product module grow at most like the power of the Frobenius degree given by the codimension.
- **claim-product-test-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eu-test-number)): On a fixed good reduction, every product module $M$ satisfies $$\label{eu:test-leading-zero} \int_X\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_c(f^*M)\mathop{\mathrm{td}}_{2n-c}(X)=0.$$ Consequently $$\label{eu:all-tests-zero} \chi(X,f^*M)= \chi\bigl(\mathcal S,\lambda_{-1}(\Omega^1_{\mathcal S})\otimes\gamma(M)\bigr)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Every product module has vanishing leading Chern–Todd number and vanishing Euler characteristic on a good reduction.
- **claim-stabilizer-parameters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-str-stabilizer-parameters)): The restriction of the ordinary ambient Kähler cotangent sheaf to $\mathcal S$ is a vector bundle faithful on every stabilizer. At a point with stabilizer $H$, one can choose smooth parameters on the completed stratum and lift them to the ambient local ring so that their span, and therefore their generated ideal, is $H$-stable. These assertions remain true for the spread diagrams in characteristics prime to all the chart-group orders.
  - Explanation: The restricted ambient cotangent sheaf is faithful on stabilizers and smooth parameters can be chosen with stabilizer-stable span.
- **claim-rectification-of-generators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-str-rectification)): Let $K$ be a field, let $A$ be an Artin local $K$-algebra with residue field $K$, and put $C=A[[t_1,\ldots,t_d]]$. Fix finite $A$-modules $E_i,F$ and submodules $Q_i\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_A(E_i,F)$. Given surjections $C^{r_i}\to Q_i\otimes_A C$, form their evaluation map $$\Phi:\bigoplus_i(E_i\otimes_A C)\otimes_C C^{r_i} \longrightarrow F\otimes_A C.$$ An automorphism of each $C^{r_i}$ makes $\Phi$ the scalar extension of a fixed map of finite $A$-modules, with possible zero columns. Its kernel, image and cokernel are consequently extensions of finite $A$-modules, are flat over $K[[t_1,\ldots,t_d]]$, and specialize exactly on setting the parameters to zero.
  - Explanation: Evaluation maps over a product ring can be rectified to constant maps extended by smooth parameters, with flat kernel and exact specialization.
- **claim-three-product-modules** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eu-three-modules)): There exist vector bundles $P_i$ on $\mathcal S_k$ with surjections $P_i\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal A_i$. For their evaluation map $$\label{eu:evaluation} Y_0=\bigoplus_iT_i\otimes P_i \xrightarrow{\ \Phi\ }T_{j_0},\qquad Z_0=\ker\Phi,$$ the source, target, kernel, image and cokernel are product modules. The kernel sequence remains exact after setting the smooth product parameters to zero, including when those parameters have been chosen equivariantly for a point stabilizer.
  - Explanation: Vector bundles surjecting onto the right ideal give an evaluation sequence whose terms are product modules and whose kernel specializes exactly.
- **claim-character-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eu-character-extraction)): At each geometric point of $\mathcal S$, in the representation ring of its full stabilizer, $$\label{eu:trivial-fiber} \gamma(M_{T_{j_0}})-\gamma(M_{Y_0})+\gamma(M_{Z_0}) =(-1)^{j_0}[\mathbf1].$$
  - Explanation: The alternating sum of the three dual-module classes equals the signed trivial representation at every geometric point.
- **claim-restriction-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-restriction-kernel)): Let $i:F\hookrightarrow X_0$ be a smooth fiber over the smooth locus of $f_0$. Then $$\label{def:kernel-equation} \ker\bigl(i^*:H^2(X_0,\mathbb C)\longrightarrow H^2(F,\mathbb C)\bigr)=e^\perp.$$
  - Explanation: The kernel of restriction of second cohomology to a smooth fiber is the orthogonal complement of the fibration class.
- **claim-fixed-base-deformation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-fixed-base)): After shrinking $D$, there is a proper holomorphic map $$\mathfrak f:\mathcal X\longrightarrow B\times D$$ over $D$ whose central fiber is $f_0$. Every $f_t:X_t\to B$ has connected fibers, and every irreducible component of every fiber is an $n$-dimensional Lagrangian subvariety. Under the chosen marking, $c_1(f_t^*H)=e$ for every $t$. For some $m>0$, the extension of $f_0^*H^m$ is relatively generated and defines these fibrations after Stein factorization.
  - Explanation: Small deformations keeping the fibration class of type one-one admit Lagrangian fibrations over the same fixed base.
- **claim-tangent-to-orbit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-tangent-to-orbit)): On a maximal chart, let $g:Y\to V$ be one of the lifted smooth source maps, and let $S\subset V$ be the orbit stratum. At every $y\in g^{-1}(S)$, $$\label{def:differential-tangency} dg_y(T_yY)\subset T_{g(y)}S.$$ The same assertion holds on each parameter fiber of the family in Proposition <a href="#def:fixed-base" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:fixed-base">48</a>.
  - Explanation: Differentials of the lifted smooth source maps land in the tangent space of the orbit stratum.
- **claim-hamiltonian-map** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-hamiltonian)): Put $\omega_t=j_t^*\sigma_t$. There is a holomorphic bundle map $$\label{def:hamiltonian-equations} \mathcal H_t:h_t^*\Omega^1_{\mathcal S}\longrightarrow T_{Q_t}, \qquad \iota_{\mathcal H_t(\theta)}\omega_t=h_t^*\theta, \qquad dh_t\circ\mathcal H_t=0.$$ It is independent of local extensions of $\theta$ to ambient base forms, and the maps are compatible with the family over $D^\circ$.
  - Explanation: Contraction with the pulled-back symplectic form gives a bundle map from stratum differentials to vertical vector fields, independent of extensions.
- **claim-large-betti-quotient-singularities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-large-betti)): If $b_2(X)\geq5$, the base $B$ has only quotient singularities.
  - Explanation: When the second Betti number is large, period variation forces diagonal Hodge cohomology of the stratum and so only quotient singularities.
- **claim-isotrivial-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-iso-exclusion)): Let $f:X\to B$ be the projective Lagrangian fibration of Section <a href="#sec:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setup">2</a>. If $b_2(X)=4$ and the polarized period map of its smooth fibers has zero variation, then every germ of $B$ is a quotient singularity.
  - Explanation: In the rank-four isotrivial case every germ of the base is a quotient singularity.
- **claim-vertical-divisor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-iso-vertical-divisor)): The Picard number of $X$ is two, and $$\mathbb T_{\mathbb Q}:=\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(X)_{\mathbb Q}^{\perp} \quad\text{satisfies}\quad \mathbb T_{\mathbb Q}\otimes_{\mathbb Q}\mathbb C =\mathbb C[\sigma]\oplus\mathbb C[\overline{\sigma}].$$ For every prime divisor $D\subset B$, there is a unique prime divisor $F_D\subset X$ dominating $D$. Near the generic point of $D$, $$f^*D=m_DF_D$$ for a positive integer $m_D$.
  - Explanation: The Picard number is two and every base prime divisor pulls back to a unique vertical prime divisor with a positive multiplicity.
- **claim-abelian-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-iso-abelian-model)): There exist a normal projective variety $T$, a finite group $G$, an $n$-dimensional abelian variety $P$, and an $n$-dimensional abelian variety $A$, with the following properties.* 1. *The map $p:T\to B$ is finite Galois with group $G$, and the main base change of $X$ is birational over $T$ to $P\times T$. The action on the first factor is by constant affine automorphisms preserving an ample line bundle on $P$.* 2. *There is a finite surjective $G$-equivariant morphism $a:T\to A$, where $G$ acts affinely on $A$. The linear actions on $P$ and $A$ have the same kernel.* 3. *Writing this kernel as $K$, and writing $A_l$ for the quotient of $A$ by the finite group of translations induced by $K$, there are finite morphisms $$T_l:=T/K \xrightarrow{\ a_l\ } A_l, \qquad T_l\xrightarrow{\ p_l\ }B.$$ The group $G_l:=G/K$ is the Galois group of $p_l$, and acts on $A_l$ with faithful linear part.
  - Explanation: A Galois cover of the base trivializes the fibration birationally to a product with a fixed abelian variety and maps finitely and equivariantly to another abelian variety.
- **claim-ramification-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-iso-ramification)): Let $D\subset B$ be a prime divisor, let $E\subset T$ be a prime divisor over $D$, and put $D_A=a(E)$. Write $$M=e_E(p),\qquad L=e_E(a)$$ for the ramification indices of the two finite maps, and let $m_D$ be the fiber multiplicity in Lemma <a href="#iso:vertical-divisor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="iso:vertical-divisor">55</a>. Then $$\label{iso:multiplicity-bound} Lm_D\le M.$$ The linear part of a generator of the cyclic inertia is either the identity or a pseudoreflection, on both $A$ and $P$. If $M_l,L_l$ are the analogous indices for $p_l,a_l$, then $$\label{iso:linear-ramification-bound} L_l\le M_l.$$
  - Explanation: Ramification indices of the two finite maps are bounded by the base ramification relative to the fiber multiplicity, and inertia acts by pseudoreflections.
- **claim-order-two-excluded** (theorem, [source](#stmt-iso-order-two)): Let $g_D$ be a local inertia generator at a prime base divisor $D$. The linear part of $g_D^{m_D}$ on $P$ does not have order two.
  - Explanation: The powered inertia generator does not act with order two, since that would force a divisor of multiplicity two on an unramified base change.
- **bound-dimension-reflection-group** (bound, [source](#stmt-iso-dimension-bound)): The linear monodromy $G_l$ acts irreducibly on $\operatorname{Lie}(P)$, is generated by pseudoreflections of order greater than two, and satisfies $n\le4$.
  - Explanation: The linear monodromy is irreducible and generated by pseudoreflections of order above two, which bounds the dimension.
- **claim-local-translation-fields** (theorem, [source](#stmt-iso-local-fields)): For $z_0\in T_l$, put $J=\operatorname{Stab}_{G_l}(z_0)$ and $$V_J=\operatorname{Lie}(A_l)^J.$$ Every translation field belonging to $V_J$ lifts to a holomorphic vector field on a neighborhood of $z_0$ in $T_l$. This lift is $J$-invariant and descends through $p_l$. If $p_l(z_0)\in S_0$, then $\dim V_J\le1$.
  - Explanation: Stabilizer-invariant translation fields lift and descend holomorphically, and along the minimal stratum their space has dimension at most one.
- **claim-regular-differentials** (theorem, [source](#stmt-iso-regular-differentials)): For every local holomorphic function $u$ on $B$, its pulled-back differential has an expression $$\label{iso:differential-coefficients} p_l^*(du)=\sum_{i=1}^n h_i\,a_l^*\alpha_i,$$ where the $\alpha_i$ are a basis of translation-invariant one-forms on $A_l$ and the $h_i$ are holomorphic on the corresponding neighborhood of $T_l$. At a point $z_0$ with stabilizer $J$, the covector $\sum_i h_i(z_0)\alpha_i$ is $J$-invariant.
  - Explanation: Pulled-back differentials of base functions have holomorphic coefficients against invariant one-forms, because ramification indices compare correctly.
- **claim-elliptic-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-iso-elliptic-lift)): Let $C$ be an irreducible component of $(p_l^{-1}(S_0))_{\mathrm{red}}$. Then $C$ is a smooth curve of genus one. Its image $E=a_l(C)$ is an elliptic translate in $A_l$, and $a_l\|_C:C\to E$ and $p_l\|_C:C\to S_0$ are étale. At every $z\in C$, $$\operatorname{Lie}(A_l)^{\operatorname{Stab}_{G_l}(z)} =\operatorname{Lie}(E).$$ Here $\operatorname{Lie}(E)$ means the constant tangent direction of the translate $E$.
  - Explanation: Each component over the stratum is a smooth curve of genus one mapping unramified to an elliptic translate and to the stratum.
- **claim-second-system** (theorem, [source](#stmt-secsys-second-system)): Arbitrarily close to the original point of $D$, there is a member $X'=X_t$ and an integral $(1,1)$-class $u$ such that $e$ and $u$ are nef and $$q(e)=q(u)=0,\qquad q(e,u)>0,$$ and the whole positive interval satisfies $$\label{secsys:geometric-target} \{a e+b u:a,b>0\}\subset\mathcal K_{X'}.$$ In particular $X'$ is projective. Its fixed-base fibration $f'=f_t:X'\to B$ admits a second projective Lagrangian fibration $g:X'\to C$, with $C$ normal and projective. For every fiber $A$ over the smooth locus of $g$, the restriction $f'\|_A:A\to B$ is finite and surjective. Thus $B$ has a finite cover by an abelian variety.
  - Explanation: Arbitrarily close to the original point there is a projective member carrying two isotropic nef classes with a Kähler positive interval, yielding a finite cover of the base by an abelian variety.
- **claim-realized-periods-open** (theorem, [source](#stmt-secsys-metric-periods)): Realized positive three-planes form an open subset. In rank greater than four, a three-plane fails to be realized precisely when it is orthogonal to a rational negative MBM class of the marked component. In rank four, the weaker assertion that every nonrealized three-plane has a rational negative orthogonal line suffices and holds. At a fixed oriented period, Kähler cones of marked models in the component are either equal or disjoint.
  - Explanation: Realized positive three-planes form an open set, with nonrealization characterized by orthogonal rational negative wall classes.
- **claim-actual-monodromy-null-rotation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-secsys-actual-monodromy)): Let $a\in U_{\mathbb Q}$ be nonzero and null. Every rational null rotation fixing $a$, extended as the identity on $U^\perp$, has a positive power that is an integral parallel-transport operator in the chosen marked component.
  - Explanation: Rational null rotations have a positive power realized as parallel transport in the marked component.
- **claim-loxodromic-operator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-secsys-loxodromic)): There is a parallel-transport operator $\gamma$ preserving $U$ and acting identically on $U^\perp$ such that its characteristic polynomial on $U$ is irreducible over $\mathbb Q$. Its real eigenvalues are $\rho,\rho^{-1}$ with $\rho>1$, and its other two eigenvalues are a nonreal conjugate pair on the unit circle. Its attracting and repelling future null lines $a_+,a_-$ can both be placed in any prescribed neighborhood of $[w_*]$ in the null boundary.
  - Explanation: An integral parallel-transport operator exists whose characteristic polynomial is irreducible and whose limit lines lie near a prescribed cusp.
- **claim-whole-geodesic-avoids-bad-set** (theorem, [source](#stmt-secsys-whole-geodesic)): For all sufficiently large integers $N$, $$(e,\gamma^N e)\cap J=\varnothing.$$
  - Explanation: For large iterates the whole geodesic segment avoids the set of nonrealized planes.
- **claim-rank-four-variation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-secsys-rank-four-variation)): If $b_2(X_0)=4$ and the polarized family of smooth fibers of $f_0$ has nonzero variation, the choices in Proposition <a href="#secsys:second-system" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="secsys:second-system">63</a> can be made so that $g$ also has nonzero polarized variation.
  - Explanation: In rank four, nonzero variation of the original family can be transported so the second fibration also varies.
- **claim-varying-case-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-exclusion)): Let $X$ be a projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold of dimension $2n$ with $b_2(X)=4$, and let $$f:X\longrightarrow B,\qquad g:X\longrightarrow C$$ be projective Lagrangian fibrations with normal projective bases. Assume that the polarized family of smooth fibers of $g$ has nonzero variation and that, for every smooth fiber $A_t$ of $g$, the restriction $f\|_{A_t}:A_t\to B$ is finite and surjective. Fix a connected minimum stratum $S_0\subset B$ and its smooth Deligne–Mumford stack $\mathcal S$ as in Propositions <a href="#str:minimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="str:minimum">23</a> and <a href="#str:products" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="str:products">25</a>. Then $$H^{a,b}(\mathcal S)=0\quad\text{for }a\ne b,\qquad \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S_0)=\sum_p h^{p,p}(\mathcal S)>0.$$
  - Explanation: With nonzero variation and finite maps from fibers, Hodge cohomology of the stratum is diagonal and its Euler characteristic is positive.
- **claim-cup-image** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-cup-image)): For every $t\in T$ there is a natural factorization $$h_t^*\Omega^1_{\mathcal S}\longrightarrow j_t^*\Omega^1_{A_t}\longrightarrow\Omega^1_{Q_t}. \label{var:factorization}$$ If $0\ne\alpha\in H^{a,b}(\mathcal S)$, its pullback is a nonzero global flat section $y$ of $U^{a+b}_\mathbb C$. Moreover, for the morphism of variations $$\mathcal T:\Lambda^a V_\mathbb Q\otimes U^b_\mathbb Q\longrightarrow U^{a+b}_\mathbb Q, \qquad v\otimes u\longmapsto j^*v\smile u, \label{var:cup-map}$$ we have, at every point of $T$, $$y_t\in\mathcal T_t\bigl( \Lambda^a H_{1,t}\otimes (U^b_t)^{0,b}\bigr). \label{var:top-cup}$$
  - Explanation: Stratum differentials factor through invariant forms of the abelian fiber, and pullback of a nonzero class is a flat section with a nonzero top cup component.
- **claim-joint-monodromy-higgs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-joint-monodromy)): Let $T$ be a connected smooth quasi-projective algebraic variety, and let $\pi:\mathcal A\to T$ and $\rho:\mathcal Q\to T$ be smooth projective morphisms. The fibers of $\rho$ may be disconnected. For an integer $b\ge0$, put $V_\mathbb Q=R^1\pi_*\mathbb Q$ and $U^b_\mathbb Q=R^b\rho_*\mathbb Q$, choose flat polarizations on these rational variations, and write $H_1=V^{1,0}$. Assume the following fixed-part condition for every pure tensor word in $V_\mathbb Q,U^b_\mathbb Q$ and their duals: its full-monodromy invariant subspace is a constant Hodge structure, so every Hodge component of a global flat invariant tensor is again globally flat. This condition holds for the geometric systems just specified, as verified below.* *Let $G$ be the full Zariski closure of the monodromy on $V_\mathbb Q\oplus U^b_\mathbb Q$. Then $G$ is linearly reductive, though it may be disconnected. Its Lie algebra $\mathfrak g_\mathbb C$, transported by conjugation as a flat subbundle of $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(V_\mathbb C)\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(U^b_\mathbb C)$, is a subvariation of Hodge structure of weight zero. For every tangent vector $x\in T_tT$, $$(\theta_{V,x},\theta_{U,x})\in\mathfrak g_t^{-1,1}. \label{var:higgs-in-lie}$$ The image $$E_t=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(\mathfrak g_t^{-1,1} \longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(H_{1,t},\overline H_{1,t})\bigr) \subset\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2 H_{1,t}^* \label{var:E}$$ is a subbundle preserved by the Chern connection. In unitary frames it satisfies $$A B^* C+C B^* A\in E_t\qquad(A,B,C\in E_t), \label{var:triple}$$ where $B^*$ is the Hermitian adjoint matrix.
  - Explanation: Higgs operators lie in the lowering part of the Lie algebra of the joint monodromy and satisfy a triple-product closure.
- **claim-maximal-rank-kernels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-matrix)): Let $H$ be a finite-dimensional Hermitian vector space and let $E\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2 H^*$ be a complex subspace satisfying <a href="#var:triple" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="var:triple">[var:triple]</a> in unitary coordinates. Suppose $c\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^3H^*$ and $c(x,-,-)\in E$ for every $x\in H$. If $A\in E$ has the maximum rank occurring in $E$, then $$c(k,-,-)=0\qquad(k\in\ker A).$$
  - Explanation: A symmetric cubic whose contractions lie in a triple-closed space vanishes on kernels of maximal-rank elements.
- **claim-invertible-lowering-matrix** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-invertible)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#var:exclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="var:exclusion">69</a>, every fiber of the bundle $E$ attached to the families $\pi,\rho$ above contains an invertible matrix.
  - Explanation: Every fiber of the Higgs-image bundle contains an invertible matrix, by irreducibility from the one-dimensional invariant space.
- **claim-smooth-base-from-abelian-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quot-smooth-base)): Let $f:X\to B$ be the original projective Lagrangian fibration in the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Suppose that $B$ has quotient singularities and that there is a finite surjective morphism $$p:A\longrightarrow B$$ from an abelian variety $A$ of dimension $n$. Then $B$ is smooth.
  - Explanation: A base with quotient singularities that admits a finite surjection from an abelian variety is smooth.
- **claim-representable-lifts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quot-lifts)): The maps $f$ and $p$ lift to representable morphisms $$\widetilde f:X\longrightarrow\mathfrak B, \qquad \widetilde p:A\longrightarrow\mathfrak B, \qquad c\widetilde f=f,\quad c\widetilde p=p.$$ The first is projective and equidimensional of relative dimension $n$, with connected fibers. The second is finite, flat, and surjective.
  - Explanation: The fibration and the abelian cover lift to representable morphisms over the canonical smooth stack.
- **claim-k-theory-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quot-k-identity)): In $K^0(\mathfrak B)$, $$\label{quot:direct-image} \widetilde f_![\mathcal O_X] =\sum_{j=0}^{n}(-1)^j[\Omega^j_{\mathfrak B}] =\lambda_{-1}(\Omega^1_{\mathfrak B}).$$ Moreover, for some integer $m>0$, $$\label{quot:nilpotence} \bigl([T_{\mathfrak B}]-n[\mathcal O_{\mathfrak B}]\bigr)^m \lambda_{-1}(\Omega^1_{\mathfrak B})=0 .$$
  - Explanation: Direct image of the structure sheaf equals the exterior class of cotangent bundle on the stack, with a nilpotence relation.
- **claim-translation-field-no-zero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quot-translation-field)): For each connected component $S$ of the nonidentity inertia of $\mathfrak B$, let $j:S\to\mathfrak B$ be its natural morphism, let $h$ denote the tautological finite-order automorphism, and set $$d=\dim S,\qquad W=A\times_{\mathfrak B}S,\qquad a:W\to A,\qquad q:W\to S,\qquad E=q^*T_S .$$ There is a translation-invariant vector field $v\in\operatorname{Lie}(A)$ such that, simultaneously for every such $S$, the section $$\label{quot:projected-section} s_{v,S}\in H^0(W,E),\qquad s_{v,S}=\operatorname{pr}_{h=1}\bigl(d\widetilde p(v)\|_W\bigr),$$ has no zero.
  - Explanation: One translation field exists whose projection onto fixed directions has no zero along every nonidentity inertia component.
- **claim-dominant-differences-avoid-inertia** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quot-nondominance)): If an irreducible component of $\mathcal R$ contains a nonidentity arrow with difference zero, its difference image is a proper subvariety of $A$.
  - Explanation: A relation component through a nonidentity zero-difference arrow has a proper difference image.
- **claim-difference-image-torsion-coset** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quot-difference-image)): Let $Z$ be the normalization of an irreducible component of $\mathcal R$, and suppose that its difference image $D_Z$ has dimension $r<n$. Then $$D_Z=\tau+D$$ for an abelian subvariety $D\subset A$ of dimension $r$ and a torsion point $\tau$. If $K=D^\perp$, the image of $Z$ in $A\times A$ is stable under diagonal translation by $K$. This action lifts uniquely to $Z$; both coordinate morphisms are equivariant, and $\delta_Z$ is invariant.
  - Explanation: A proper difference image is a torsion translate of an abelian subvariety and the component is invariant under the complementary translations.
- **claim-ramification-residual-component** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quot-ramification)): Let $D\subsetneq A$ be an abelian subvariety and $K=D^\perp$. There is a prime component $L$ of the ramification divisor of $\widetilde p:A\to\mathfrak B$ which maps surjectively to $A/K$. For such an $L$, there is one irreducible component $\mathcal R'\ne i(A)$ of the actual relation $\mathcal R$ such that $$i(L)\subset\mathcal R', \qquad \delta(\mathcal R')\not\subset D .$$
  - Explanation: Ampleness of the ramification divisor gives a component dominating the quotient and a residual relation component with difference outside the subvariety.
- **claim-marked-divisor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quot-marked-divisor)): Let $\nu:Z\to\mathcal R$ be the normalization of an irreducible component followed by inclusion, with difference image an abelian subvariety $D\subsetneq A$, and put $K=D^\perp$. Suppose $z_0\in Z$ lies over a nonidentity arrow of difference zero. For $L$ as in Lemma <a href="#quot:ramification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="quot:ramification">80</a>, there are $k\in K$, $e_0=k\cdot z_0$, and a reduced prime divisor $E\subset Z$ through $e_0$, such that $x_0:=s(e_0)=t(e_0)\in L$, $\nu(e_0)$ is nonidentity, and $$s(E)=L,\qquad \delta_Z(E)=D .$$
  - Explanation: A translate of the marked arrow lies on a prime divisor mapping onto the ramification component with difference image the subvariety.

      "explanation": "Collects the known structure of the given fibration: projectivity, Fano-type klt base, reflexive identification of higher direct images with reflexive differentials, projective-space Betti numbers, and projective space when the base is smooth.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-maximal-charts",
        "exact_quote": "Every pointed germ $(B,b)$ has a connected finite normal Galois quasi-étale cover $(C,c)$ which dominates every connected finite quasi-étale cover of that germ. The chart $(C,c)$ is klt, is finitely dominated by a smooth germ, and has no nontrivial connected finite quasi-étale cover. The finite diagrams can be defined over an algebraic étale neighborhood of $b$.*\n\n*More generally, let $U\\to B$ be an integral separated étale algebraic neighborhood and let $\\pi:C\\to U$ be an integral finite normal quasi-étale cover. Put $X_U=X\\times_B U$. The base change $X_U\\times_U C$ has a unique irreducible component dominating $C$; let $Z$ be its normalization and $h:Z\\to C$ its structure map. Then $h$ is projective and equidimensional of relative dimension $n$, has connected fibers, and satisfies $h_*\\mathcal O_Z=\\mathcal O_C$. The finite map $p:Z\\to X_U$ is étale. Consequently $Z$ is smooth, the pullback of the symplectic form on $X$ is symplectic on $Z$, its canonical bundle is trivial, and the fibers of $h$ are Lagrangian. If $\\pi$ is Galois with group $\\Gamma$, then $p$ is a $\\Gamma$-torsor. Ordinary étale base change on $C$ preserves projectivity, equidimensionality, connected fibers, the equality $h_*\\mathcal O_Z=\\mathcal O_C$, and the smooth symplectic source. The torsor in the statement belongs to the original finite chart.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-trace-radical",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(R,\\mathfrak m)$ be any noetherian normal henselian local $\\mathbb C$-algebra with residue field $\\mathbb C$. For finite reflexive modules $M,N$, define $$\\mathcal R(M,N)=\n \\{a\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_R(M,N):\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(ba)\\in\\mathfrak m\n                 \\text{ for every }b\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_R(N,M)\\}.$$ These spaces form a tensor ideal. For every $M$, $\\mathcal R(M,M)$ is the Jacobson radical of the finite $R$-algebra $\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_R(M)$. The quotient category is semisimple abelian over $\\mathbb C$, has finite-dimensional Hom spaces, and its categorical dimensions are the positive integer ranks of its nonzero objects.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-loc-trace-radical",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(R,\\mathfrak m)$ be any noetherian normal henselian local $\\mathbb C$-algebra with residue field $\\mathbb C$. For every $M$, $\\mathcal R(M,M)$ is the Jacobson radical of the finite $R$-algebra $\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_R(M)$. The quotient category is semisimple abelian over $\\mathbb C$, has finite-dimensional Hom spaces, and its categorical dimensions are the positive integer ranks of its nonzero objects.",
          "source_id": "PM-041.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(R,\\mathfrak m)$ be any noetherian normal henselian local $\\mathbb C$-algebra with residue field $\\mathbb C$.         "anchor": "stmt-loc-split-lift",
        "exact_quote": "Over any ring in Lemma <a href=\"#loc:trace-radical\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"loc:trace-radical\">9</a>, if a morphism of reflexive modules becomes a split injection in the trace quotient, it is already a split injection of reflexive modules. An isomorphism in the quotient lifts to an isomorphism. In particular, an object is a sum of copies of the tensor unit in the quotient if and only if its underlying module is free.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-atiyah-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be an object, and let $t_j$ be actual sections of finitely many functorial mixed tensor powers $T_j(M)$ of $M$ and $M^\\vee$ on the chart. Direct sums and the usual symmetric or alternating projectors may be used; each construction carries the natural action of every endomorphism of $M$. Put $Q=\\bigoplus_jT_j(M)$ and define $$\\Phi_M:\\underline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}}(M)\\longrightarrow Q,\n       \\qquad A\\longmapsto(A\\cdot t_j)_j$$ by the infinitesimal tensor action. The coefficient map for the Atiyah class is the morphism in $\\mathcal C$ $$\\label{loc:psi-coefficient}\n \\Psi_M=\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_V\\widehat\\otimes\\Phi_M:\n V\\widehat\\otimes\\underline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}}(M)\n       \\longrightarrow V\\widehat\\otimes Q.$$ On the smooth locus its ordinary bundle restriction satisfies $(\\Psi_{M,U})_*a(M_U)=0$ in $H^1(U,(V\\widehat\\otimes Q)_U)$. If $\\overline{\\Psi_M}$ is injective in $\\overline{\\mathcal C}$, then $a(M_U)=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-loc-atiyah-tests",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be an object, and let $t_j$ be actual sections of finitely many functorial mixed tensor powers $T_j(M)$ of $M$ and $M^\\vee$ on the chart. Direct sums and the usual symmetric or alternating projectors may be used; each construction carries the natural action of every endomorphism of $M$. Put $Q=\\bigoplus_jT_j(M)$ and define $$\\Phi_M:\\underline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}}(M)\\longrightarrow Q,\n       \\qquad A\\longmapsto(A\\cdot t_j)_j$$ by the infinitesimal tensor action. The coefficient map for the Atiyah class is the morphism in $\\mathcal C$ $$\\label{loc:psi-coefficient}\n \\Psi_M=\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_V\\widehat\\otimes\\Phi_M:\n V\\widehat\\otimes\\underline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}}(M)\n       \\longrightarrow V\\widehat\\otimes Q.$$ On the smooth locus its ordinary bundle restriction satisfies $(\\Psi_{M,U})_*a(M_U)=0$ in $H^1(U,(V\\widehat\\otimes Q)_U)$. If $\\overline{\\Psi_M}$ is injective in $\\overline{\\mathcal C}$, then $a(M_U)=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-041.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $M$ be an object, and let $t_j$ be actual sections of finitely many functorial mixed tensor powers $T_j(M)$ of $M$ and $M^\\vee$ on the chart. Direct sums and the usual symmetric or alternating projectors may be used; each construction carries the natural action of every endomorphism of $M$. Put $Q=\\bigoplus_jT_j(M)$ and define $$\\Phi_M:\\underline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}}(M)\\longrightarrow Q,\n       \\qquad A\\longmapsto(A\\cdot t_j)_j$$ by the infinitesimal tensor action. The coefficient map for the Atiyah class is the morphism in $\\mathcal C$ $$\\label{loc:psi-coefficient}\n \\Psi_M=\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_V\\widehat\\otimes\\Phi_M:\n V\\widehat\\otimes\\underline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}}(M)\n       \\longrightarrow V\\widehat\\otimes Q.$$ On the smooth locus its ordinary bundle restriction satisfies $(\\Psi_{M,U})_*a(M_U)=0$ in $H^1(U,(V\\widehat\\otimes Q)_U)$. If $\\overline{\\Psi_M}$ is injective in $\\overline{\\mathcal C}$, then $a(M_U)=0$.",

        "exact_quote": "Lift a decomposition of $V_{\\mathrm{fib}}$ into irreducible $G$-modules to a decomposition $V=\\bigoplus_iV_i$ of reflexive modules.         "anchor": "stmt-loc-slsp",
        "exact_quote": "For a maximal chart there is a decomposition $$V_{\\mathrm{fib}}={\\bf1}^{\\oplus s}\\oplus\\bigoplus_{i=1}^r L_i,\n \\qquad\n G=\\prod_{i=1}^rG_i,\n \\qquad\n G_i=\\operatorname{SL}(L_i)\\ \\text{or}\\\n       \\operatorname{Sp}(L_i),\n \\label{loc:group-product}$$ where each $G_i$ acts by its standard representation on $L_i$ and trivially on all other summands.         "anchor": "stmt-str-evaluation",
        "exact_quote": "The largest rank of a free direct summand of $\\Omega_V^{[1]}{}_v$ is $r(v)$. Moreover, if $\\beta$ is a reflexive one-form and a vector field $\\xi$ has zero evaluation at $v$, then $\\beta(\\xi)(v)=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Let $d=\\min_{b\\in B}s(b)$. If $d=n$, every germ of $B$ is a quotient singularity. Otherwise the minimum locus is a nonempty closed algebraic subset. On a maximal chart over a point of $S_0$, its reduced inverse image is, as a germ of a pair, $$\\label{str:minimum-pair}\n (V,S_V)\\simeq(\\Delta^d\\times T,\\Delta^d\\times\\{t\\}).$$ At every point of $S_V$, the vector-field evaluation space is exactly the tangent space to $S_V$. There is a smooth separated Deligne–Mumford stack $\\mathcal S$ with coarse space $S_0$ whose charts are these smooth factors.         "anchor": "stmt-str-marked-artin",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(V,v)$ be an algebraic complex germ, let $S\\subset V$ be a reduced closed subgerm, and suppose the analytic pair is $(\\Delta^d\\times T,\\Delta^d\\times\\{t\\})$. There are an algebraic transverse germ $(T_0,t_0)$ and common étale neighborhoods of pairs $$\\label{str:etale-product-pair}\n (V,S)\\ \\longleftarrow\\ (U,S_U)\\ \\longrightarrow\\\n (\\mathbb A^d\\times T_0,\\mathbb A^d\\times\\{t_0\\}).$$ Both inverse-image ideals defining $S_U$ are equal, without truncation. For $k\\geq1$, put $A_k=\\mathcal O_{T_0,t_0}/\\mathfrak m_{t_0}^k$ and let $U_k$ be defined by the $k$th power of this common ideal. After shrinking the displayed neighborhoods, there are compatible isomorphisms $$\\label{str:finite-product}\n U_k\\simeq S_U\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}A_k\n \\quad\\text{over}\\quad \\mathbb A^d\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}A_k.$$ No equivariance of these product isomorphisms is asserted.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-str-products",
        "exact_quote": "The following data exist along $S_0$.*\n\n1.  *There are finitely many normal finite Galois quasi-étale charts $V_\\alpha\\to B_\\alpha$, with deck groups $\\Gamma_\\alpha$ and with $B_\\alpha\\to B$ separated and étale, covering $S_0$. They are maximal at every point over $S_0$.*\n\n2.  *Normalized main overlaps give an étale groupoid along the reduced inverse images $S_\\alpha\\subset V_\\alpha$. Its quotient is the stack $\\mathcal S$ of Proposition <a href=\"#str:minimum\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"str:minimum\">23</a>. Writing $I$ for the marked ideal on the object charts, the same finite diagrams define, for every $k\\geq1$, a proper separated Deligne–Mumford stack $\\mathcal S_k$, with $\\mathcal S_1=\\mathcal S$. Its coarse space is finite over $B$ and projective.*\n\n3.  *A finite family of étale refinements of the object charts covers their closed strata and has the marked-product presentations of Lemma <a href=\"#str:marked-artin\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"str:marked-artin\">24</a>. All transition maps come from the normalized ambient overlaps and preserve $I$ exactly. Their restrictions are consequently compatible for all powers $I^k$.*\n\n4.  *The normalized source charts give ordinary pullback and descent of coherent sheaves on any $\\mathcal S_k$ to coherent sheaves on $X$ supported over $S_0$.*\n\n*These assertions are encoded by finitely many algebraic schemes, morphisms, ideals and identities. In particular they can be spread simultaneously before choosing any order of Frobenius pullback.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-str-uniform",
        "exact_quote": "On the finite family of product refinements in Proposition <a href=\"#str:products\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"str:products\">25</a>, the ambient sheaves $\\mathcal N_j=\\Omega^{[j]}$, the fixed natural mixed reflexive tensor expressions in them, and their ambient Hom and trace maps have fixed transverse descriptions. Their completed systems at order $k$ are obtained from fixed finite modules over $A_k$ by extension along the smooth factor. The same holds for kernels and images of the resulting fixed transverse maps. Residual retracts are obtained by applying their idempotents to the resulting barred bundles. In particular the barred Hom sheaves in <a href=\"#str:barred-hom\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"str:barred-hom\">[str:barred-hom]</a> are locally free on $\\mathcal S$ and their fibers are the local residual trace Hom spaces.*\n\n*Consequently a property of these modules and maps that, for each fixed transverse complete ring, holds at every order $k\\geq k_0$ has one valid order on the whole stratum, provided it is preserved by flat scalar extension and can be checked by faithfully flat completion. The completed product descriptions persist after replacing the smooth parameters by lifts of another coordinate system on the reduced stratum.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-str-direct-images",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U\\to B$ be an integral separated étale algebraic neighborhood, let $\\pi:C\\to U$ be an integral finite normal Galois quasi-étale chart, and let $h:Z\\to C$ be the normalized source chart of Proposition <a href=\"#loc:maximal-charts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"loc:maximal-charts\">8</a>. For the global degreewise isomorphisms $\\phi_j:R^jf_*\\mathcal O_X\\to\\Omega_B^{[j]}$ fixed after Proposition <a href=\"#prop:background\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:background\">2</a>, there are unique isomorphisms $$\\phi_{j,C}:R^jh_*\\mathcal O_Z\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\Omega_C^{[j]},\n \\qquad 0\\leq j\\leq n,$$ whose restrictions to $C^\\circ=C\\times_BB_{\\mathrm{reg}}$ are the ordinary flat pullbacks of $\\phi_j$. The degree-zero map is the unit. These isomorphisms are compatible with ordinary étale refinement and with every normalized main overlap whose projections are étale. In particular they give compatible ambient identifications $\\mathcal N_{j,\\alpha}=R^jh_{\\alpha*}\\mathcal O_{X_\\alpha}$ on the charts of Proposition <a href=\"#str:products\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"str:products\">25</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

For the global degreewise isomorphisms $\\phi_j:R^jf_*\\mathcal O_X\\to\\Omega_B^{[j]}$ fixed after Proposition <a href=\"#prop:background\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:background\">2</a>, there are unique isomorphisms $$\\phi_{j,C}:R^jh_*\\mathcal O_Z\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\Omega_C^{[j]},\n \\qquad 0\\leq j\\leq n,$$ whose restrictions to $C^\\circ=C\\times_BB_{\\mathrm{reg}}$ are the ordinary flat pullbacks of $\\phi_j$. The degree-zero map is the unit. These isomorphisms are compatible with ordinary étale refinement and with every normalized main overlap whose projections are étale. In particular they give compatible ambient identifications $\\mathcal N_{j,\\alpha}=R^jh_{\\alpha*}\\mathcal O_{X_\\alpha}$ on the charts of Proposition <a href=\"#str:products\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"str:products\">25</a>.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bc-finite-modules",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a complete noetherian normal local domain, and let $A_1,A_2$ be finite dominant local $L$-algebras that are regular local rings. Put $d_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}_L A_i$. If these rings have positive characteristic and arise by completing local rings of varieties over a perfect field, then $$\\label{bc:regular-comparison}\n                 A_1^{\\oplus d_2}\\simeq A_2^{\\oplus d_1}\n                 \\qquad\\text{as $L$-modules}.$$ The same conclusion holds in characteristic zero when the diagram is the completion of an algebraic finite diagram at a closed point over $\\mathbb C$. Finite products of the indicated local algebras can also be compared, using their total generic ranks.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bc-finite-modules",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a complete noetherian normal local domain, and let $A_1,A_2$ be finite dominant local $L$-algebras that are regular local rings. Finite products of the indicated local algebras can also be compared, using their total generic ranks.",
          "source_id": "PM-041.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bc-uniform-twists",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be noetherian, let $\\mathsf H$ be an algebra finite over its central subring $R$, and let $\\mathcal P$ on $Y=\\mathbb P^n_R$ be locally finite projective as a right $\\mathsf H_Y$-module. There is an integer $m_0$ such that, for every $m\\ge m_0$ and every left $\\mathsf H$-module $Q$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n H^j(Y,\\mathcal P(m)\\otimes_{\\mathsf H}Q)&=0\n                     &&(j>0),\\label{bc:uniform-vanishing}\\\\\n H^0(Y,\\mathcal P(m))\\otimes_{\\mathsf H}Q\n       &\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n H^0(Y,\\mathcal P(m)\\otimes_{\\mathsf H}Q).\n                     \\label{bc:uniform-sections}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The arrow is the canonical multiplication map, and $H^0(Y,\\mathcal P(m))$ is a finite projective right $\\mathsf H$-module. The bound is independent of $Q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bc-uniform-twists",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be noetherian, let $\\mathsf H$ be an algebra finite over its central subring $R$, and let $\\mathcal P$ on $Y=\\mathbb P^n_R$ be locally finite projective as a right $\\mathsf H_Y$-module. The bound is independent of $Q$.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $R$ be noetherian, let $\\mathsf H$ be an algebra finite over its central subring $R$, and let $\\mathcal P$ on $Y=\\mathbb P^n_R$ be locally finite projective as a right $\\mathsf H_Y$-module. There is an integer $m_0$ such that, for every $m\\ge m_0$ and every left $\\mathsf H$-module $Q$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n H^j(Y,\\mathcal P(m)\\otimes_{\\mathsf H}Q)&=0\n                     &&(j>0),\\label{bc:uniform-vanishing}\\\\\n H^0(Y,\\mathcal P(m))\\otimes_{\\mathsf H}Q\n       &\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n H^0(Y,\\mathcal P(m)\\otimes_{\\mathsf H}Q).\n                     \\label{bc:uniform-sections}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The arrow is the canonical multiplication map, and $H^0(Y,\\mathcal P(m))$ is a finite projective right $\\mathsf H$-module. The bound is independent of $Q$.",
      "explanation": "One twist bound gives vanishing of higher cohomology and global-section base change for all coefficient modules at once.",

        "anchor": "stmt-bc-differential-identity",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed $g\\ne0$ in <a href=\"#bc:cleared-homotopy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bc:cleared-homotopy\">[bc:cleared-homotopy]</a>, on a finite principal-open cover of $P$ there are finitely many $t_j\\in R$ and algebraic differential operators $D_j$ with regular coefficients on the respective open sets such that $$\\label{bc:differential-equation}\n                  \\eta=\\sum_j D_j(g t_j\\eta).$$ The identity is in the middle relative de Rham module on the generic smooth locus, but its operators are regular also at the other points of the chosen open sets.         "anchor": "stmt-bc-ordinary",
        "exact_quote": "Perform the finite characteristic-zero preparations above on a finite-type integral model. After any fixed finite enlargement of its algebraic and coherent data, and restriction to any nonempty principal open retaining the required identities and geometric properties, one can choose a characteristic-zero closed point and a number-field model with geometric reductions in arbitrarily large residue characteristics $p$ that retain these data and satisfy $$\\psi_X(1)=\\delta\\in k^*,\\qquad\n p>e_\\mu,\\quad p>o_g\n       \\quad\\text{on every member of the finite chart cover}.$$ On each such reduction the following assertions hold simultaneously. On every retained source chart, for every $R$-module $M$ and every $i$, the canonical map $$\\label{bc:canonical-base-change}\n (R^ih_*\\mathcal O_Z)\\otimes_RM\\longrightarrow\n                          R^ih_*(h^*\\widetilde M)$$ is an isomorphism, with ordinary tensor and pullback.         "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },

In particular, for every coherent module $M$ supported on any finite thickening of $\\mathcal S$ in $\\widehat{\\mathcal B}$, $$\\label{bc:ordinary-euler}\n \\chi(X,f^*M)=\\sum_{i=0}^n(-1)^i\n                \\chi(\\widehat{\\mathcal B},N_i\\otimes M).$$ One reduction works for all these $M$ and all thickening orders.",

        "exact_quote": "In the barred tensor calculus, the trivial-isotypic multiplicity bundle of $V=N_1$ is canonically $$\\mathcal U=\\overline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}}(\\mathcal O,V)=\\Omega^1_{\\mathcal S}.$$ Let $W$ be the complementary object to this evaluation summand. There are idempotents $p_b\\in\\overline{\\mathcal E}$, supported in the $N_b$ block, whose images are $\\Lambda^bW$. If $L$ is any left $\\overline{\\mathcal E}$-module, then $$\\label{eu:block-exterior}\n \\mathbf1_{N_i}L\\simeq\n \\bigoplus_{a+b=i}\\Lambda^a\\Omega^1_{\\mathcal S}\\otimes p_bL.$$ Moreover, there are an integer $j_0$ and an isotypic idempotent $u$ in the $N_{j_0}$ block such that $$\\label{eu:isolated-constituent}\n u\\overline{\\mathcal E}p_b=\n \\begin{cases}\n   \\mathcal O_{\\mathcal S}u,&b=j_0,\\\\\n   0,&b\\ne j_0.\n \\end{cases}$$ The identity line in this formula has trivial action under every stabilizer.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-eu-tame-functional",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal D$ be a smooth proper tame Deligne–Mumford stack of pure dimension $d$ with projective coarse space. The functional $$\\Theta(G)=\\chi\\bigl(\\mathcal D,\n                 \\lambda_{-1}(\\Omega^1_{\\mathcal D})\\otimes G\\bigr)$$ on vector bundles is additive, and its additive extension to $K^0(\\mathcal D)$ depends only on pointwise full-stabilizer representations. There is a constant $C_{\\mathcal D}$ such that $|\\Theta(G)|\\le C_{\\mathcal D}\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(G)$ for every vector bundle $G$. Consequently, for any finite signed presentation $V=\\sum_i a_i[G_i]$, one has $$|\\Theta(V)|\\le C_{\\mathcal D}\\sum_i|a_i|\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}(G_i).$$ The latter bound uses the chosen presentation, not the virtual rank.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-eu-tame-functional",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal D$ be a smooth proper tame Deligne–Mumford stack of pure dimension $d$ with projective coarse space.
